Notes per system: see the note column of each <system>.tsv here.

How it was run:

| system | version | durability at acknowledgement | loader |
|---|---|---|---|
| ClickHouse (server) | 26.8.2.7 | not fsynced at acknowledgement (fsync_after_insert 0, the default); syncfs after the load, timed | ch_load.sh: one RowBinary INSERT through clickhouse client (native protocol); merges settled |
| DuckDB (embedded) | 1.5.6 | CHECKPOINT after the load (the WAL is fsynced at commit) | embq duckdb load: 4 appenders |
| InfluxDB 2 (InfluxQL and Flux) | 2.7.12 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (the default) | loadlp: line protocol over HTTP /api/v2/write, 5000 lines per request, 8 connections |
| InfluxDB 3 Core | 3.12.0-core | acknowledged once the WAL file of the flush interval (1 s) is written to the object store (local files); fsync not verified here | loadlp: line protocol over HTTP /api/v3/write_lp, 100000 lines per request, 16 connections |
| Apache IoTDB (table model, CHIMP + LZMA2; sizes only) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes |
| Apache IoTDB (table model, CHIMP + ZSTD; sizes only) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes |
| Apache IoTDB (table model, GORILLA + LZMA2; sizes only) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes |
| Apache IoTDB (table model, ZSTD) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes |
| Apache IoTDB (table model) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes |
| Prometheus | v3.15.0 | remote write: acknowledged from the head (WAL written, not fsynced per request); backfill: blocks written by promtool, then sync | phase 4: promtool tsdb create-blocks-from openmetrics (backfill) and loadprw remote write |
| QuestDB (Parquet partitions, ZSTD) | 10.0.1 | not fsynced at acknowledgement (cairo.commit.mode nosync, the default); WAL applied and sync after the load, timed | loadlp: InfluxDB line protocol over HTTP /write, 100000 lines per request, 8 connections |
| QuestDB (native partitions) | 10.0.1 | not fsynced at acknowledgement (cairo.commit.mode nosync, the default); WAL applied and sync after the load, timed | loadlp: InfluxDB line protocol over HTTP /write, 100000 lines per request, 8 connections |
| TDengine (community, xz; sizes only) | 3.4.2.8 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (WAL_LEVEL 2, WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0; default WAL_LEVEL 1) | loadlp: line protocol through taosAdapter /influxdb/v1/write (schemaless), 100000 lines per request, 8 connections. TDengine's documented fastest path (stmt over WebSocket) is not used, so its load time is NOT comparable: leave TDengine out of any load-speed ranking |
| TDengine (community, ZSTD) | 3.4.2.8 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (WAL_LEVEL 2, WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0; default WAL_LEVEL 1) | loadlp: line protocol through taosAdapter /influxdb/v1/write (schemaless), 100000 lines per request, 8 connections. TDengine's documented fastest path (stmt over WebSocket) is not used, so its load time is NOT comparable: leave TDengine out of any load-speed ranking |
| TDengine (community) | 3.4.2.8 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (WAL_LEVEL 2, WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0; default WAL_LEVEL 1) | loadlp: line protocol through taosAdapter /influxdb/v1/write (schemaless), 100000 lines per request, 8 connections. TDengine's documented fastest path (stmt over WebSocket) is not used, so its load time is NOT comparable: leave TDengine out of any load-speed ranking |
| TimescaleDB | 2.30.1-pg17 | fsync at commit (synchronous_commit on, the default) | timescaledb-parallel-copy: CSV, 16 workers, batches of 10000 rows; then compression of every chunk |
| tsdb (tsdbd) | tsdbd 0.9.0 (build 98c4588) | WAL fsynced before every acknowledgement (group commit); large inserts as parts, fsynced | tsdbc-bench load: RowBinary input, 4 connections, compressed packed inserts (protocol 10) |
| VictoriaMetrics (single node) | v1.154.0 | acknowledged from memory (flushed within 5 s); force_flush and sync after the load, timed | loadprw: Prometheus remote write, 10000 samples per request, 8 shards |

Notes:

- ClickHouse (server): image docker.io/clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.8.2.7@sha256:fa394da808cc53f76d0344429421d6c422a6ee85fe7450135c0e3cff4df9bcbb; settings: listen_host 127.0.0.1; server defaults otherwise; MergeTree PARTITION BY toYYYYMM(ts) ORDER BY (series, ts); DoubleDelta / Gorilla + ZSTD(1); phase 1 also: default LZ4, OPTIMIZE FINAL, and ZSTD(22) after OPTIMIZE FINAL (ClickHouse's smallest lossless setting).
- DuckDB (embedded): image libduckdb-linux-amd64.zip /var/tmp/dbcmp/duckdb/v1.5.6 sha256 b845005f5132a7d8180057c35e14a7626632258782f871a90861b19c1c03841b; settings: threads 32, memory_limit 24GB; table (series INTEGER, ts TIMESTAMP_MS, value FLOAT), no key; rows sorted by series and time before loading (not counted).
- InfluxDB 2 (InfluxQL and Flux): image docker.io/library/influxdb:2.7.12@sha256:b8d940ca9376f85118260f5b6bd236b8a00b1749c3350c5578d4cde8e27f31f2; settings: http-bind-address 127.0.0.1:8086; storage-compact-full-write-cold-duration 1m; container memory 24g; settle: bench/dbcmp/i2_settle.sh.
- InfluxDB 3 Core: image docker.io/library/influxdb:3.12.0-core@sha256:624d69bca6bf6fb174aca5a974e1d96e5c5486994e6ae35bc9b4fe02b9520076; settings: --http-bind 127.0.0.1:8181 --without-auth; --object-store file; --exec-mem-pool-size 8GB --file-cache-size 4GB --force-snapshot-mem-size 10GB --query-file-limit 1000000; container memory 28g; queried once Parquet is written (snapshot settled).
- Apache IoTDB (table model, CHIMP + LZMA2; sizes only): image docker.io/apache/iotdb:2.0.11-standalone@sha256:5ace0f3eceff892dd349d02288224c966f3fdeb11d08c4f575b642a110b82338; settings: wal_mode=SYNC; compaction_write_throughput_mb_per_sec=0; query_timeout_threshold=3600000; REST on 18080 published on 127.0.0.1 only (pasta, rootless Podman's port forwarding: the server binds every address; its per-request cost is measured on VictoriaMetrics: p2/vm vm.pasta.point.* against vm.point.*); heap -Xmx16G, direct 4G, container 24g; default_float_encoding=CHIMP, compressor=LZMA2 (lossless; never TS_2DIFF/RLE on floats, which round to float_precision).
- Apache IoTDB (table model, CHIMP + ZSTD; sizes only): image docker.io/apache/iotdb:2.0.11-standalone@sha256:5ace0f3eceff892dd349d02288224c966f3fdeb11d08c4f575b642a110b82338; settings: wal_mode=SYNC; compaction_write_throughput_mb_per_sec=0; query_timeout_threshold=3600000; REST on 18080 published on 127.0.0.1 only (pasta, rootless Podman's port forwarding: the server binds every address; its per-request cost is measured on VictoriaMetrics: p2/vm vm.pasta.point.* against vm.point.*); heap -Xmx16G, direct 4G, container 24g; default_float_encoding=CHIMP, compressor=ZSTD (lossless; never TS_2DIFF/RLE on floats, which round to float_precision).

- TDengine (community): image docker.io/tdengine/tsdb:3.4.2.8@sha256:44f58e6f9b013bccb94c4a27dd0ec7bca2982474fd3b390cafb48f709db1f0b1; settings: CREATE DATABASE ... WAL_LEVEL 2 WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0 PRECISION 'ms'; phase 2 with VGROUPS 16 (default 2; TDengine's planning docs: a core serves 1 to 2 vnodes); taosKeeper, taosExplorer, taosX off; REST on 6041 published on 127.0.0.1 only (pasta, rootless Podman's port forwarding: the server binds every address; its per-request cost is measured on VictoriaMetrics: p2/vm vm.pasta.point.* against vm.point.*); value FLOAT: ENCODE bss COMPRESS lz4 LEVEL medium (default); container memory 24g.
- TimescaleDB: image docker.io/timescale/timescaledb:2.30.1-pg17@sha256:c79fa5891443d1cdf6de5258d4c2bdb8cde29052c45df2062b13d351e0202005; settings: listen_addresses 127.0.0.1; timescaledb-tune 24GB / 32 CPUs; work_mem: timescaledb-tune's for the plain server; 256MB for the timescaledb-ha server of TWA and LTTB (tune's 6 MB spills TWA's window sorts); container memory limit 24 GB (TS_MEM): 32 clients of 100-series batches grew to ~1.7 GB per backend at either work_mem; an OOM kill is reported as such; max_wal_size 16GB; /dev/shm 8 GB (Podman's default 64 MB fails parallel queries); hypertable, 1-day chunks, compressed (segmentby series, orderby ts); TWA and LTTB of the toolkit run in the timescaledb-ha image.
- tsdb (tsdbd): image /var/tmp/rb3-src at 98c4588; settings: defaults (wal_sync_ms 0, 1-day partitions, 8192-row granules); --listen 127.0.0.1 --port 0 --port-file; also measured after merges (tsdb_optimize: one part per partition).
- VictoriaMetrics (single node): image docker.io/victoriametrics/victoria-metrics:v1.154.0@sha256:d83112326f700fe65f6fe66b12348406191cb20826ae0df72d676a2e69d3f3c9; settings: -retentionPeriod=100y; -search.disableCache; -search.maxQueryDuration=3600s -search.maxQueueDuration=3600s; -precisionBits 64 (default; ~12 significant digits); --network host, -httpListenAddr=127.0.0.1:8428.

## Phase 2: the mixed table, server systems

In batch classes (NAME.bK.cN, K windows per request) each line says what its q/s counts: requests in qdrive's and relq's lines (every system but tsdbq), windows in tsdbq's, whose requests per second are added in brackets. KIND.b100.c1 of tsdb is its whole-range TWA of every series in one request (QUERY_MULTI), the form one SQL query has in the other systems. Rows marked p6: are from the extra pass (run.sh p6), on a load of their own.

Memory sweep (run.sh p6: one load, then every class above once, per core left out; one run each, not averaged): the server's memory per class, without / with the page cache (sampled every 0.5 s). The peak is what the server held at most during the step, memory earlier classes left behind included (caches, pools); the rise is how far it grew above what it held when the step started.

| class (one run) | peak during the step (includes what earlier classes left), without / with page cache | rise over the step's start, without / with page cache | flags |
|---|---|---|---|
| load | rb-qdb 1.34 / 5.19 GB | rb-qdb 0.81 / 4.51 GB |  |

The twa1d / twa7d.cN rows (relq) time a fixed set of 1000 windows from start to end, connects included, and give windows/s from that wall time; the other systems' twa1d / twa7d rows (and tsdb's KIND.bK.cN) are q/s over a 15 s steady-state run, so the two are not the same measure.

Memory sweep (run.sh p6: one load, then every class above once, per core left out; one run each, not averaged): the server's memory per class, without / with the page cache (sampled every 0.5 s). The peak is what the server held at most during the step, memory earlier classes left behind included (caches, pools); the rise is how far it grew above what it held when the step started.

Extra pass (p6) on 2026-11-02 07:04: REPS=5 runs per TWA batch class (p6/*.mean.txt); every other class once into memsweep/ (peak memory only, never averaged); tsdb tsdbd 0.9.0 (build 98c4588), /var/tmp/rb5-src at ?; harness /var/tmp/rb5-src at ?. the extra pass loads the data once more: its TWA batch timings come from that load, not from the load of phase 2's rows.


How it was run:

| system | version | durability at acknowledgement | loader | query client | connections / threads | one query, one batch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ClickHouse (server) | 26.8.2.7 | not fsynced at acknowledgement (fsync_after_insert 0, the default); syncfs after the load, timed | ch_load.sh: one RowBinary INSERT through clickhouse client (native protocol); merges settled | qdrive over HTTP (port 8123, TabSeparated replies), the same driver and client wall time as every other HTTP system; beside it 'server only': the server's query_log in microseconds. Not clickhouse-benchmark: it rounds latencies to whole ms and its 1/QPS has a fixed 100 ms wait | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 query on max_threads 32 (default) | one SQL statement per series; a batch of K series is one SQL statement: K UNION ALL branches tagged by a member column (mkbatch.py), sent as one request |
| InfluxDB 2 (InfluxQL and Flux) | 2.7.12 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (the default) | loadlp: line protocol over HTTP /api/v2/write, 5000 lines per request, 8 connections | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); HTTP (InfluxQL /query and Flux /api/v2/query) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one query per series; a batch of K: InfluxQL K statements joined by ';' in one request, Flux K yields in one request |
| InfluxDB 3 Core | 3.12.0-core | acknowledged once the WAL file of the flush interval (1 s) is written to the object store (local files); fsync not verified here | loadlp: line protocol over HTTP /api/v3/write_lp, 100000 lines per request, 16 connections | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); HTTP SQL (/api/v3/query_sql) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one SQL statement per series; a batch of K series is one SQL statement: K UNION ALL branches tagged by a member column (mkbatch.py), sent as one request |
| Apache IoTDB (table model, ZSTD) | 2.0.11-standalone | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (wal_mode SYNC; default ASYNC) | iotload.py: Python TableSession tablets over the native RPC (6667), 100000 rows per tablet, 8 processes | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); REST /rest/table/v1/query | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one SQL statement per series; a batch of K series is one SQL statement: K UNION ALL branches tagged by a member column (mkbatch.py), sent as one request |

| TDengine (community) | 3.4.2.8 | WAL fsynced before acknowledgement (WAL_LEVEL 2, WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0; default WAL_LEVEL 1) | loadlp: line protocol through taosAdapter /influxdb/v1/write (schemaless), 100000 lines per request, 8 connections. TDengine's documented fastest path (stmt over WebSocket) is not used, so its load time is NOT comparable: leave TDengine out of any load-speed ranking | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); REST /rest/sql (taosAdapter) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one SQL statement per series; a batch of K series is one SQL statement: K UNION ALL branches tagged by a member column (mkbatch.py), sent as one request |
| TimescaleDB | 2.30.1-pg17 | fsync at commit (synchronous_commit on, the default) | timescaledb-parallel-copy: CSV, 16 workers, batches of 10000 rows; then compression of every chunk | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); PostgreSQL wire protocol | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 query, and per series on 16 connections | one SQL statement per series; a batch of K series is one SQL statement: K UNION ALL branches tagged by a member column (mkbatch.py), sent as one request |
| tsdb (tsdbd) | tsdbd 0.9.0 (build 98c4588) | WAL fsynced before every acknowledgement (group commit); large inserts as parts, fsynced | tsdbc-bench load: RowBinary input, 4 connections, compressed packed inserts (protocol 10) | tsdbq (C client library, tsdb's binary protocol over TCP; protocol 10) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 connection (and per series on 16); TWA/LTTB 1 | one request per series; a batch of K series is one QUERY_MULTI request with K series |
| VictoriaMetrics (single node) | v1.154.0 | acknowledged from memory (flushed within 5 s); force_flush and sync after the load, timed | loadprw: Prometheus remote write, 10000 samples per request, 8 shards | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); Prometheus HTTP API (MetricsQL): aggr_over_time computes count, sum, min and max in one pass; query_range in pieces of up to 30,000 steps (-search.maxPointsPerTimeseries) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one HTTP query per series; a batch of K is K requests joined in one qdrive line (no multi-series query with per-series windows in PromQL): those K requests run one after another on one connection, so a batch's time is their sum; tsdb's batch runs its K series in parallel. Compare throughput by the per-series time at 32 clients, not by the batch time |

Notes:

- ClickHouse (server): image docker.io/clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.8.2.7@sha256:fa394da808cc53f76d0344429421d6c422a6ee85fe7450135c0e3cff4df9bcbb; settings: listen_host 127.0.0.1; server defaults otherwise; MergeTree PARTITION BY toYYYYMM(ts) ORDER BY (series, ts); DoubleDelta / Gorilla + ZSTD(1); phase 1 also: default LZ4, OPTIMIZE FINAL, and ZSTD(22) after OPTIMIZE FINAL (ClickHouse's smallest lossless setting); container memory limit 48g for every server, tsdbd too (SERVER_MEM: a cgroup, no swap; a server that outgrows it is killed by the kernel and that is a result: REFUSED.txt, flags in the tables); phase 2: settled when no merge runs and the part count is unchanged for 180 s (parts per partition before and after the suites in parts-*.txt); beside the main rows, 'merged.*' rows after OPTIMIZE FINAL (one part per partition, like tsdb's merged rows).
  Per core: max_threads=1; the server's process tree pinned to CPU 2 by taskset (rootless Podman has no cpuset controller), the client on CPU 3. Warm-ups: whole range: one warm-up run, then REPS runs of the one query; random-window classes: 3 s warm-up on 1 connection, then REPS runs of SECS s per connection count; LTTB/TWA: one warm-up, then REPS runs. Runs: REPS=5 runs per measurement (mean, sd, min-max in *.mean.txt); SECS=15 s per random-window run; batches of 1 10 100 series.
- InfluxDB 2 (InfluxQL and Flux): image docker.io/library/influxdb:2.7.12@sha256:b8d940ca9376f85118260f5b6bd236b8a00b1749c3350c5578d4cde8e27f31f2; settings: http-bind-address 127.0.0.1:8086; storage-compact-full-write-cold-duration 1m; container memory 24g; settle: bench/dbcmp/i2_settle.sh.
  Per core: a restart with GOMAXPROCS=1 (influxd has no query-thread knob); the server's process tree pinned to CPU 2 by taskset (rootless Podman has no cpuset controller), the client on CPU 3. Warm-ups: whole range: one warm-up run, then REPS runs of the one query; random-window classes: 3 s warm-up on 1 connection, then REPS runs of SECS s per connection count; LTTB/TWA: one warm-up, then REPS runs. Runs: REPS=5 runs per measurement (mean, sd, min-max in *.mean.txt); SECS=15 s per random-window run; batches of 1 10 100 series.
  '-' for twad: no query in rel.py for i2flux.
  '-' for twal: InfluxDB 2's timeWeightedAvg interpolates only between the bucket's own rows, not across its edges (a third of the buckets differ from tsdb's LINEAR TWA); not timed.

WAL_LEVEL 2 WAL_FSYNC_PERIOD 0 PRECISION 'ms'; phase 2 with VGROUPS 16 (default 2; TDengine's planning docs: a core serves 1 to 2 vnodes); taosKeeper, taosExplorer, taosX off; REST on 6041 published on 127.0.0.1 only (pasta, rootless Podman's port forwarding: the server binds every address; its per-request cost is measured on VictoriaMetrics: p2/vm vm.pasta.point.* against vm.point.*); value FLOAT: ENCODE bss COMPRESS lz4 LEVEL medium (default); container memory 48g.

How it was run:

| system | version | durability at acknowledgement | loader | query client | connections / threads | one query, one batch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prometheus | v3.15.0 | remote write: acknowledged from the head (WAL written, not fsynced per request); backfill: blocks written by promtool, then sync | phase 4: promtool tsdb create-blocks-from openmetrics (backfill) and loadprw remote write | qdrive (C, one connection per client thread; times each request from send to the last byte of the answer); Prometheus HTTP API (PromQL) | random-window classes 1 and 32; whole range 1 | one HTTP query per series |

- Prometheus: image docker.io/prom/prometheus:v3.15.0@sha256:efd719c99d83b060d9daefdcf00360461adf279f45ef5391f8d111892118753e; settings: --web.listen-address 127.0.0.1; --web.enable-remote-write-receiver; --query.max-concurrency=32; bench/dbcmp/prometheus.yml.
  Per core: not measured. Warm-ups: whole range: one warm-up run, then REPS runs of the one query; random-window classes: 3 s warm-up on 1 connection, then REPS runs of SECS s per connection count; LTTB/TWA: one warm-up, then REPS runs. Runs: REPS=5 runs per measurement (mean, sd, min-max in *.mean.txt); SECS=15 s per random-window run; batches of 1 10 100 series.
